1→"""Execution strategy engine for narrative lanes and parallelization hints."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Iterable
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.intelligence.narrative._constants import (
     9→    _DETECTOR_CASCADE,
    10→    STRUCTURAL_MERGE,
    11→)
    12→
    13→
    14→def open_files_by_detector(issues: dict[str, dict[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, set[str]]:
    15→    """Collect file sets of open issues by detector."""
    16→    by_detector: dict[str, set[str]] = {}
    17→    for issue in issues.values():
    18→        if issue["status"] != "open" or issue.get("suppressed"):
    19→            continue
    20→        detector = issue.get("detector", "unknown")
    21→        if detector in STRUCTURAL_MERGE:
    22→            detector = "structural"
    23→        file_path = issue.get("file", "")
    24→        if not file_path:
    25→            by_detector.setdefault(detector, set())
    26→            continue
    27→        by_detector.setdefault(detector, set()).add(file_path)
    28→    return by_detector
    29→
    30→
    31→def compute_fixer_leverage(
    32→    by_detector: dict[str, int],
    33→    actions: list[dict[str, Any]],
    34→    phase: str,
    35→    _lang: str | None,
    36→) -> dict[str, float | int | str]:
    37→    """Estimate how much value automated fixers would deliver."""
    38→    auto_fixable = sum(
    39→        action.get("count", 0) for action in actions if action.get("type") == "auto_fix"
    40→    )
    41→    total = sum(by_detector.values())
    42→    coverage = auto_fixable / total if total > 0 else 0.0
    43→    total_impact = sum(action.get("impact", 0) for action in actions)
    44→    auto_impact = sum(
    45→        action.get("impact", 0)
    46→        for action in actions
    47→        if action.get("type") == "auto_fix"
    48→    )
    49→    impact_ratio = auto_impact / total_impact if total_impact > 0 else 0.0
    50→
    51→    if coverage == 0:
    52→        recommendation = "none"
    53→    elif coverage > 0.4 or impact_ratio > 0.3:
    54→        recommendation = "strong"
    55→    elif phase in ("first_scan", "stagnation", "regression") and coverage > 0.15:
    56→        recommendation = "strong"
    57→    elif coverage > 0.1:
    58→        recommendation = "moderate"
    59→    else:
    60→        recommendation = "none"
    61→
    62→    return {
    63→        "auto_fixable_count": auto_fixable,
    64→        "total_count": total,
    65→        "coverage": round(coverage, 3),
    66→        "impact_ratio": round(impact_ratio, 3),
    67→        "recommendation": recommendation,
    68→    }
    69→
    70→
    71→def _cleanup_lane_actions(actions: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    72→    cascade_rank = {detector: idx for idx, detector in enumerate(_DETECTOR_CASCADE)}
    73→
    74→    def sort_key(action: dict[str, Any]) -> tuple[int, float]:
    75→        detector = action.get("detector", "")
    76→        return (cascade_rank.get(detector, 99), -action.get("impact", 0))
    77→
    78→    return sorted(actions, key=sort_key)
    79→
    80→
    81→def _files_for_actions(
    82→    actions: Iterable[dict[str, Any]], files_by_detector: dict[str, set[str]]
    83→) -> set[str]:
    84→    files: set[str] = set()
    85→    for action in actions:
    86→        detector = action.get("detector")
    87→        if detector and detector in files_by_detector:
    88→            files |= files_by_detector[detector]
    89→    return files
    90→
    91→
    92→def _group_by_file_overlap(
    93→    action_files: list[tuple[dict[str, Any], set[str]]],
    94→) -> list[list[tuple[dict[str, Any], set[str]]]]:
    95→    """Group actions whose file sets overlap using union-find."""
    96→    item_count = len(action_files)
    97→    if item_count == 0:
    98→        return []
    99→
   100→    parent = list(range(item_count))
   101→
   102→    def find(index: int) -> int:
   103→        while parent[index] != index:
   104→            parent[index] = parent[parent[index]]
   105→            index = parent[index]
   106→        return index
   107→
   108→    def union(left: int, right: int) -> None:
   109→        left_root, right_root = find(left), find(right)
   110→        if left_root != right_root:
   111→            parent[left_root] = right_root
   112→
   113→    for left in range(item_count):
   114→        for right in range(left + 1, item_count):
   115→            if action_files[left][1] & action_files[right][1]:
   116→                union(left, right)
   117→
   118→    grouped_indices: dict[int, list[int]] = {}
   119→    for index in range(item_count):
   120→        grouped_indices.setdefault(find(index), []).append(index)
   121→
   122→    return [
   123→        [action_files[index] for index in indices]
   124→        for indices in grouped_indices.values()
   125→    ]
   126→
   127→
   128→def _refactor_lanes(
   129→    refactor_actions: list[dict[str, Any]],
   130→    files_by_detector: dict[str, set[str]],
   131→) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
   132→    """Split refactor work into independent lanes by file overlap."""
   133→    lanes: dict[str, dict] = {}
   134→    action_files = [
   135→        (action, files_by_detector.get(action.get("detector"), set()))
   136→        for action in refactor_actions
   137→    ]
   138→
   139→    test_coverage_actions = [
   140→        (action, files)
   141→        for action, files in action_files
   142→        if action.get("detector") == "test_coverage"
   143→    ]
   144→    other_actions = [
   145→        (action, files)
   146→        for action, files in action_files
   147→        if action.get("detector") != "test_coverage"
   148→    ]
   149→
   150→    groups = _group_by_file_overlap(other_actions)
   151→    for index, group in enumerate(groups):
   152→        lane_name = f"refactor_{index}" if len(groups) > 1 else "refactor"
   153→        lanes[lane_name] = {
   154→            "actions": [action["priority"] for action, _ in group],
   155→            "file_count": len(
   156→                _files_for_actions((action for action, _ in group), files_by_detector)
   157→            ),
   158→            "total_impact": round(
   159→                sum(action.get("impact", 0) for action, _ in group), 1
   160→            ),
   161→            "automation": "manual",
   162→            "run_first": False,
   163→        }
   164→
   165→    if test_coverage_actions:
   166→        lanes["test_coverage"] = {
   167→            "actions": [action["priority"] for action, _ in test_coverage_actions],
   168→            "file_count": len(
   169→                _files_for_actions(
   170→                    (action for action, _ in test_coverage_actions), files_by_detector
   171→                )
   172→            ),
   173→            "total_impact": round(
   174→                sum(action.get("impact", 0) for action, _ in test_coverage_actions), 1
   175→            ),
   176→            "automation": "manual",
   177→            "run_first": False,
   178→        }
   179→
   180→    return lanes
   181→
   182→
   183→def compute_lanes(
   184→    actions: list[dict[str, Any]],
   185→    files_by_detector: dict[str, set[str]],
   186→) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
   187→    """Partition actions into parallelizable work lanes."""
   188→    lanes: dict[str, dict] = {}
   189→
   190→    cleanup_actions = _cleanup_lane_actions(
   191→        [action for action in actions if action.get("type") == "auto_fix"]
   192→    )
   193→    reorganize_actions = [
   194→        action for action in actions if action.get("type") == "reorganize"
   195→    ]
   196→    debt_actions = [action for action in actions if action.get("type") == "debt_review"]
   197→    refactor_actions = [
   198→        action
   199→        for action in actions
   200→        if action.get("type") not in {"auto_fix", "reorganize", "debt_review"}
   201→    ]
   202→
   203→    if cleanup_actions:
   204→        cleanup_files = _files_for_actions(cleanup_actions, files_by_detector)
   205→        lanes["cleanup"] = {
   206→            "actions": [action["priority"] for action in cleanup_actions],
   207→            "file_count": len(cleanup_files),
   208→            "total_impact": round(
   209→                sum(action.get("impact", 0) for action in cleanup_actions), 1
   210→            ),
   211→            "automation": "full",
   212→            "run_first": False,
   213→        }
   214→
   215→    if reorganize_actions:
   216→        lanes["restructure"] = {
   217→            "actions": [action["priority"] for action in reorganize_actions],
   218→            "file_count": len(
   219→                _files_for_actions(reorganize_actions, files_by_detector)
   220→            ),
   221→            "total_impact": round(
   222→                sum(action.get("impact", 0) for action in reorganize_actions), 1
   223→            ),
   224→            "automation": "manual",
   225→            "run_first": False,
   226→        }
   227→
   228→    if refactor_actions:
   229→        lanes.update(_refactor_lanes(refactor_actions, files_by_detector))
   230→
   231→    if debt_actions:
   232→        lanes["debt_review"] = {
   233→            "actions": [action["priority"] for action in debt_actions],
   234→            "file_count": 0,
   235→            "total_impact": 0.0,
   236→            "automation": "manual",
   237→            "run_first": False,
   238→        }
   239→
   240→    if "cleanup" in lanes:
   241→        for lane_name, lane in lanes.items():
   242→            if lane_name == "cleanup":
   243→                continue
   244→            lane_files = _files_for_actions(
   245→                (action for action in actions if action["priority"] in lane["actions"]),
   246→                files_by_detector,
   247→            )
   248→            if cleanup_files & lane_files:
   249→                lanes["cleanup"]["run_first"] = True
   250→                break
   251→
   252→    return lanes
   253→
   254→
   255→def _significant_lane(lane_name: str, lane: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   256→    if lane_name == "debt_review" or lane.get("run_first"):
   257→        return False
   258→    return lane.get("file_count", 0) >= 5 or lane.get("total_impact", 0) >= 1.0
   259→
   260→
   261→def compute_strategy_hint(
   262→    fixer_leverage: dict[str, Any],
   263→    lanes: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
   264→    can_parallelize: bool,
   265→    phase: str,
   266→) -> str:
   267→    """Generate one- or two-sentence execution strategy guidance."""
   268→    recommendation = fixer_leverage.get("recommendation", "none")
   269→    coverage_pct = round(fixer_leverage.get("coverage", 0) * 100)
   270→    lane_count = sum(
   271→        1
   272→        for lane_name, lane in lanes.items()
   273→        if lane_name != "debt_review" and not lane.get("run_first")
   274→    )
   275→
   276→    if recommendation == "strong" and can_parallelize:
   277→        return (
   278→            f"Run fixers first — they cover {coverage_pct}% of issues. "
   279→            f"Then {lane_count} independent workstreams, safe to parallelize. "
   280→            "Rescan after each phase to verify."
   281→        )
   282→    if recommendation == "strong":
   283→        return (
   284→            f"Run fixers first — they cover {coverage_pct}% of issues. "
   285→            "Then rescan to verify."
   286→        )
   287→    if can_parallelize:
   288→        return (
   289→            f"{lane_count} independent workstreams, safe to parallelize. "
   290→            "Rescan after each phase to verify."
   291→        )
   292→    if phase == "maintenance":
   293→        return "Maintenance mode — address new issues as they appear."
   294→    if phase == "stagnation":
   295→        return "Try a different dimension to break the plateau."
   296→    return "Work through actions in priority order. Rescan after each fix to track progress."
   297→
   298→
   299→def compute_strategy(
   300→    issues: dict[str, dict[str, Any]],
   301→    by_detector: dict[str, int],
   302→    actions: list[dict[str, Any]],
   303→    phase: str,
   304→    lang: str | None,
   305→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   306→    """Orchestrate strategy computation and annotate actions with lanes."""
   307→    files_by_detector = open_files_by_detector(issues)
   308→    fixer_leverage = compute_fixer_leverage(by_detector, actions, phase, lang)
   309→    lanes = compute_lanes(actions, files_by_detector)
   310→
   311→    action_lane: dict[int, str] = {}
   312→    for lane_name, lane in lanes.items():
   313→        for priority in lane["actions"]:
   314→            action_lane[priority] = lane_name
   315→    for action in actions:
   316→        action["lane"] = action_lane.get(action["priority"])
   317→
   318→    significant_non_blocked = [
   319→        (lane_name, lane)
   320→        for lane_name, lane in lanes.items()
   321→        if _significant_lane(lane_name, lane)
   322→    ]
   323→    can_parallelize = len(significant_non_blocked) >= 2
   324→
   325→    hint = compute_strategy_hint(fixer_leverage, lanes, can_parallelize, phase)
   326→    review_action = next(
   327→        (action for action in actions if action.get("detector") == "review"), None
   328→    )
   329→    if review_action:
   330→        hint += f" Review: {review_action['count']} issue(s) — `desloppify show review --status open`."
   331→
   332→    return {
   333→        "fixer_leverage": fixer_leverage,
   334→        "lanes": {name: {**lane} for name, lane in lanes.items()},
   335→        "can_parallelize": can_parallelize,
   336→        "hint": hint,
   337→    }
   338→

Perfect! I have gathered extensive information. Let me now create a comprehensive summary report of my findings.

## Summary: How Desloppify Presents Structural Issues to Users and Agents

Based on my thorough exploration of the desloppify codebase, here's how structural (LOC/complexity) issues are presented:

### 1. **Structural Detector & Finding Generation**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/base/structural.py` (lines 24-79)

The structural detector merges four sub-detectors (large, complexity, gods, concerns) and generates findings with this **exact summary language**:

```python
summary = "Needs decomposition: " + " / ".join(signals)
```

**Example outputs:**
- "Needs decomposition: large file (800 LOC) / too many public methods"
- "Needs decomposition: complexity score 95 / monster functions"

**Tiering logic:**
- **T4 (High)**: 3+ signals → "high" confidence  
- **T3 (Medium)**: 1-2 signals → "medium" confidence

**Key thresholds:**
- Default LOC threshold: **500 lines** (from `large.py` line 13)
- Complexity-only files: minimum score of **35** to be flagged (tunable)

### 2. **Cluster Description Strategy**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/cluster_strategy.py` (lines 88-92)

When auto-clustering structural issues, the system generates this cluster description:

```python
if detector == "structural":
    files = {os.path.basename(member.get("file", "")) for member in members}
    if len(files) == 1:
        return f"Decompose {next(iter(files))}"
    return f"Decompose {count} large files"
```

**Cluster display examples:**
- Single file: "Decompose config.py"
- Multiple files: "Decompose 5 large files"

### 3. **Detector Metadata & Framing**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/base/registry.py` (lines 197-203)

Structural issues are officially classified as:

```python
"structural": DetectorMeta(
    "structural",
    "structural",
    "File health",  # ← dimension name
    "refactor",     # ← action_type
    "decompose large files — extract logic into focused modules",  # ← guidance text
    needs_judgment=True,  # ← MARKED AS REQUIRING JUDGMENT
    tier=3,
)
```

**Key attributes:**
- **needs_judgment=True**: Flagged as requiring design judgment (not auto-fixable)
- **action_type="refactor"**: Positioned as refactoring work (not automation)
- **guidance**: "decompose large files — extract logic into focused modules"

### 4. **How Structural Issues Are Presented to Agents (Narrative System)**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/action_engine.py` (lines 174-207)

Structural issues are presented in action descriptions like:

```python
description = f"{count} {detector} issues — {guidance}"
# → e.g., "3 structural issues — decompose large files — extract logic into focused modules"
```

**Refactor lane classification:**
- Structural work is grouped into "**refactor**" lanes (not "cleanup" or "auto_fix")
- Marked as **manual work** with parallel execution hints

### 5. **Terminal Output ("next" Command)**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/next/render.py` (line 304)

Issues are displayed with:

```python
print(f"  {colorize(item.get('summary', ''), 'yellow')}")
# Yellow-highlighted: "Needs decomposition: large file (800 LOC) / too many methods"
```

**Confidence display:** "(medium confidence)" or "(high confidence)" printed in bold above the summary.

### 6. **Status/Area Workflow**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/status/render_structural.py` (lines 73-82)

When structural debt is significant, the system suggests:

```
Workflow:
  1. desloppify show <area> --status wontfix --top 50
  2. Create tasks/<date>-<area-name>.md with decomposition plan
  3. Farm each task doc to a sub-agent for implementation
```

This shows structural work is **explicitly designed for agent hand-off**.

### 7. **Narrative Coaching Language**

**Files:** 
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/reminders.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/headline.py`

**Note:** Structural issues are **NOT** flagged with urgent reminders. They fall into the "refactor" lane which is typically lower priority than auto-fixable issues.

---

## Key Findings on Tone & Framing

1. **Language Used:**
   - "**Needs decomposition**" — declarative, prescriptive
   - "**Decompose**" — imperative verb in cluster titles
   - "**extract logic into focused modules**" — guided direction

2. **Aggressive vs. Advisory:**
   - **DECLARATIVE**: The summary "Needs decomposition" is a statement, not "Consider decomposing"
   - **JUDGMENT-GATED**: The `needs_judgment=True` flag ensures issues are NOT treated as clear-cut facts; they require human design review
   - **REFACTOR-CLASSIFIED**: Grouped as "refactor" work (lowest automation tier), signaling these are judgment calls, not mechanical problems

3. **Confidence Nuance:**
   - Issues with 3+ signals: "**high** confidence" (objectively complex)
   - Issues with 1-2 signals: "**medium** confidence" (borderline cases)
   - This suggests the tool acknowledges uncertainty

4. **Agent Communication:**
   - Status workflow explicitly directs users to "create task docs" and "farm to sub-agents"
   - Structural issues are **intentionally surfaced for human/agent judgment** rather than automated fixing
   - The "decomposition plan" is meant to be a **collaborative design doc**, not an automated instruction

---

## Recommendation on Framing

The current language ("Needs decomposition") is **moderately aggressive**. It crosses from advisory ("could benefit from") to prescriptive ("needs to"). A more balanced approach that preserves judgment-gating might be:

- **Current**: "Needs decomposition: large file (800 LOC)"
- **More advisory**: "Consider decomposing: large file (800 LOC)"
- **Hybrid**: Keep the current summary but emphasize in coaching that "decomposition is optional for files under 1000 LOC unless structural signals compound"

The system **is** well-designed to prevent over-confidence: the `needs_judgment=True` flag and refactor-lane classification ensure agents know these are judgment calls, not dictates.

The desloppify plan has 32 clusters and ~270 queued items. The top 20 items are ALL structural decomposition ("Needs decomposition: large file (420 LOC)") which is causing agents to mechanically split files into facade wrappers — making the codebase worse.

I need to use desloppify's own plan management commands to reorganize. Available commands:
- `desloppify plan skip <id> --note "<why>"` — skip individual items
- `desloppify plan skip <cluster_name> --note "<why>"` — skip entire cluster
- `desloppify plan cluster reorder <names> <position>` — reorder clusters (top, bottom, before, after)
- `desloppify plan cluster reorder <names> --item <pattern> <position>` — reorder within clusters

## Task 2: Tone down the structural issue presentation

The structural detector in `desloppify/languages/_framework/base/structural.py` generates summaries like:
```python
summary = "Needs decomposition: " + " / ".join(signals)
```

And cluster descriptions in `desloppify/engine/_plan/cluster_strategy.py`:
```python
if detector == "structural":
    return f"Decompose {count} large files"
```

And guidance in `desloppify/base/registry.py`:
```
"decompose large files — extract logic into focused modules"
```

This language is too prescriptive. "Needs decomposition" reads as a command, which is why agents mechanically execute it. It should be advisory since `needs_judgment=True` is set.

Changes needed:
1. `structural.py`: Change "Needs decomposition" → "Consider splitting" or "Large file"
2. `cluster_strategy.py`: Change "Decompose X" → "Review file size: X" 
3. `registry.py`: Change guidance to something like "review large files — split only when responsibilities are clearly separable"

Design the specific sequence of desloppify CLI commands to execute Task 1, and the specific code edits for Task 2. Be concrete about command order — skip the noise first, then reorder the good stuff to the top.

1→"""Structural signal helpers shared across language phase implementations."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import resolve_path
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import count_lines
     9→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_project_root
    10→from desloppify.state import Issue, make_issue
    11→
    12→
    13→def add_structural_signal(structural: dict, file: str, signal: str, detail: dict):
    14→    """Add a complexity signal to the per-file structural dict.
    15→
    16→    Accumulates signals per file so they can be merged into tiered issues.
    17→    """
    18→    f = resolve_path(file)
    19→    structural.setdefault(f, {"signals": [], "detail": {}})
    20→    structural[f]["signals"].append(signal)
    21→    structural[f]["detail"].update(detail)
    22→
    23→
    24→def merge_structural_signals(
    25→    structural: dict,
    26→    stderr_fn,
    27→    *,
    28→    complexity_only_min: int = 35,
    29→) -> list[Issue]:
    30→    """Convert per-file structural signals into tiered issues.
    31→
    32→    3+ signals -> T4/high (needs decomposition).
    33→    1-2 signals -> T3/medium.
    34→    Complexity-only files (no large/god signals) need score >= complexity_only_min
    35→    to be flagged — lower complexity in small files is normal, not decomposition-worthy.
    36→    """
    37→    results = []
    38→    suppressed = 0
    39→    for filepath, data in structural.items():
    40→        if "loc" not in data["detail"]:
    41→            try:
    42→                p = (
    43→                    Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else get_project_root() / filepath
    44→                )
    45→                data["detail"]["loc"] = count_lines(p)
    46→            except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    47→                data["detail"]["loc"] = 0
    48→
    49→        # Suppress complexity-only issues below the elevated threshold.
    50→        signals = data["signals"]
    51→        is_complexity_only = all(s.startswith("complexity") for s in signals)
    52→        if is_complexity_only:
    53→            score = data["detail"].get("complexity_score", 0)
    54→            if score < complexity_only_min:
    55→                suppressed += 1
    56→                continue
    57→
    58→        signal_count = len(signals)
    59→        tier = 4 if signal_count >= 3 else 3
    60→        confidence = "high" if signal_count >= 3 else "medium"
    61→        summary = "Needs decomposition: " + " / ".join(signals)
    62→        results.append(
    63→            make_issue(
    64→                "structural",
    65→                filepath,
    66→                "",
    67→                tier=tier,
    68→                confidence=confidence,
    69→                summary=summary,
    70→                detail=data["detail"],
    71→            )
    72→        )
    73→    if suppressed:
    74→        stderr_fn(
    75→            "         "
    76→            f"{suppressed} complexity-only files below threshold (< {complexity_only_min})"
    77→        )
    78→    stderr_fn(f"         -> {len(results)} structural issues")
    79→    return results
    80→
    81→
    82→__all__ = ["add_structural_signal", "merge_structural_signals"]
    83→

1→"""Pure grouping/description/action strategy for plan auto-clustering."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.registry import DetectorMeta
     8→
     9→AUTO_PREFIX = "auto/"
    10→
    11→
    12→def extract_subtype(issue: dict) -> str | None:
    13→    """Extract the subtype/kind from a issue."""
    14→    detail = issue.get("detail") or {}
    15→    kind = detail.get("kind")
    16→    if kind:
    17→        return kind
    18→
    19→    issue_id = issue.get("id", "")
    20→    parts = issue_id.split("::")
    21→    if len(parts) >= 3:
    22→        candidate = parts[-1]
    23→        if "/" not in candidate and "." not in candidate:
    24→            return candidate
    25→    return None
    26→
    27→
    28→def grouping_key(issue: dict, meta: DetectorMeta | None) -> str | None:
    29→    """Compute a deterministic grouping key for a issue."""
    30→    detector = issue.get("detector", "")
    31→
    32→    if meta is None:
    33→        return f"detector::{detector}"
    34→
    35→    if detector in ("review", "subjective_review"):
    36→        detail = issue.get("detail") or {}
    37→        dimension = detail.get("dimension", "")
    38→        if dimension:
    39→            return f"review::{dimension}"
    40→        return f"detector::{detector}"
    41→
    42→    if meta.needs_judgment and detector in ("structural", "responsibility_cohesion"):
    43→        file_path = issue.get("file", "")
    44→        if file_path:
    45→            basename = os.path.basename(file_path)
    46→            return f"file::{detector}::{basename}"
    47→
    48→    if meta.needs_judgment:
    49→        subtype = extract_subtype(issue)
    50→        if subtype:
    51→            return f"typed::{detector}::{subtype}"
    52→
    53→    if meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and not meta.needs_judgment:
    54→        return f"auto::{detector}"
    55→
    56→    return f"detector::{detector}"
    57→
    58→
    59→def cluster_name_from_key(key: str) -> str:
    60→    """Convert a grouping key to an ``auto/...`` cluster name."""
    61→    parts = key.split("::")
    62→    if len(parts) == 2:
    63→        prefix_type = parts[0]
    64→        if prefix_type == "review":
    65→            return f"{AUTO_PREFIX}review-{parts[1]}"
    66→        return f"{AUTO_PREFIX}{parts[1]}"
    67→    if len(parts) == 3:
    68→        return f"{AUTO_PREFIX}{parts[1]}-{parts[2]}"
    69→    return f"{AUTO_PREFIX}{key.replace('::', '-')}"
    70→
    71→
    72→def generate_description(
    73→    cluster_name: str,
    74→    members: list[dict],
    75→    meta: DetectorMeta | None,
    76→    subtype: str | None,
    77→) -> str:
    78→    """Generate a human-readable cluster description."""
    79→    _ = cluster_name
    80→    count = len(members)
    81→    detector = members[0].get("detector", "") if members else ""
    82→
    83→    if detector in ("review", "subjective_review"):
    84→        detail = (members[0].get("detail") or {}) if members else {}
    85→        dimension = detail.get("dimension", detector)
    86→        return f"Address {count} {dimension} review issues"
    87→
    88→    if detector == "structural":
    89→        files = {os.path.basename(member.get("file", "")) for member in members}
    90→        if len(files) == 1:
    91→            return f"Decompose {next(iter(files))}"
    92→        return f"Decompose {count} large files"
    93→
    94→    display = meta.display if meta else detector
    95→    if subtype:
    96→        label = subtype.replace("_", " ")
    97→        return f"Fix {count} {label} issues"
    98→
    99→    if meta and meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and not meta.needs_judgment:
   100→        return f"Remove {count} {display} issues"
   101→
   102→    return f"Fix {count} {display} issues"
   103→
   104→
   105→def subtype_has_fixer(meta: DetectorMeta, subtype: str | None) -> str | None:
   106→    """Check if a subtype maps to a detector fixer."""
   107→    if not meta.fixers or not subtype:
   108→        return None
   109→    fixer_name = subtype.replace("_", "-")
   110→    if fixer_name in meta.fixers:
   111→        return fixer_name
   112→    for fixer in meta.fixers:
   113→        if subtype in fixer:
   114→            return fixer
   115→    return None
   116→
   117→
   118→def strip_guidance_examples(guidance: str) -> str:
   119→    """Strip subtype examples from guidance text."""
   120→    if " — " in guidance:
   121→        return guidance.split(" — ", 1)[0].strip()
   122→    return guidance
   123→
   124→
   125→_ACTION_TYPE_TEMPLATES: dict[str, str] = {
   126→    "reorganize": "reorganize with desloppify move",
   127→    "refactor": "review and refactor each issue",
   128→    "manual_fix": "review and fix each issue",
   129→}
   130→
   131→
   132→def generate_action(
   133→    meta: DetectorMeta | None,
   134→    subtype: str | None,
   135→) -> str:
   136→    """Generate an action string from detector metadata."""
   137→    if meta is None:
   138→        return "review and fix each issue"
   139→
   140→    if subtype and meta.fixers:
   141→        matched_fixer = subtype_has_fixer(meta, subtype)
   142→        if matched_fixer:
   143→            return f"desloppify autofix {matched_fixer} --dry-run"
   144→    elif meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and meta.fixers and not meta.needs_judgment:
   145→        return f"desloppify autofix {meta.fixers[0]} --dry-run"
   146→
   147→    if meta.tool == "move":
   148→        return "desloppify move"
   149→
   150→    if meta.guidance:
   151→        if subtype:
   152→            return strip_guidance_examples(meta.guidance)
   153→        return meta.guidance
   154→
   155→    return _ACTION_TYPE_TEMPLATES.get(meta.action_type, "review and fix each issue")
   156→
   157→
   158→__all__ = [
   159→    "cluster_name_from_key",
   160→    "generate_action",
   161→    "generate_description",
   162→    "grouping_key",
   163→    "strip_guidance_examples",
   164→]
   165→

   382→_DISPLAY_ORDER = list(DISPLAY_ORDER)
   383→
   384→JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
   385→    name for name, meta in DETECTORS.items() if meta.needs_judgment
   386→)
   387→_BASE_DETECTORS: dict[str, DetectorMeta] = dict(DETECTORS)
   388→_BASE_DISPLAY_ORDER: list[str] = list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   389→_BASE_JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(JUDGMENT_DETECTORS)
   390→
   391→_on_register_callbacks: list[Callable[[], None]] = []
   392→
   393→
   394→def on_detector_registered(callback: Callable[[], None]) -> None:
   395→    """Register a callback invoked after register_detector(). No-arg."""
   396→    _on_register_callbacks.append(callback)
   397→
   398→
   399→def register_detector(meta: DetectorMeta) -> None:
   400→    """Register a detector at runtime (used by generic plugins)."""
   401→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   402→    DETECTORS[meta.name] = meta
   403→    if meta.name not in _DISPLAY_ORDER:
   404→        _DISPLAY_ORDER.append(meta.name)
   405→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = frozenset(
   406→        name for name, m in DETECTORS.items() if m.needs_judgment
   407→    )
   408→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   409→        cb()
   410→
   411→
   412→def reset_registered_detectors() -> None:
   413→    """Reset runtime-added detector registrations to built-in defaults."""
   414→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   415→    DETECTORS.clear()
   416→    DETECTORS.update(_BASE_DETECTORS)
   417→    _DISPLAY_ORDER.clear()
   418→    _DISPLAY_ORDER.extend(_BASE_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   419→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = _BASE_JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   420→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   421→        cb()
   422→
   423→
   424→def detector_names() -> list[str]:
   425→    """All registered detector names, sorted."""
   426→    return sorted(DETECTORS.keys())
   427→
   428→
   429→def display_order() -> list[str]:
   430→    """Canonical display order for terminal output."""
   431→    return list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   432→
   433→
   434→_ACTION_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
   435→_ACTION_LABELS = {
   436→    "auto_fix": "autofix",
   437→    "reorganize": "move",
   438→    "refactor": "refactor",
   439→    "manual_fix": "manual",
   440→}
   441→
   442→
   443→def dimension_action_type(dim_name: str) -> str:
   444→    """Return a compact action type label for a dimension based on its detectors."""
   445→    best = "manual"
   446→    best_pri = 99
   447→    for d in DETECTORS.values():
   448→        if d.dimension == dim_name:
   449→            pri = _ACTION_PRIORITY.get(d.action_type, 99)
   450→            if pri < best_pri:
   451→                best_pri = pri
   452→                best = d.action_type
   453→    return _ACTION_LABELS.get(best, "manual")
   454→
   455→
   456→def detector_tools() -> dict[str, dict]:
   457→    """Build detector tool metadata keyed by detector name."""
   458→    result = {}
   459→    for name, d in DETECTORS.items():
   460→        entry: dict = {
   461→            "fixers": list(d.fixers),
   462→            "action_type": d.action_type,
   463→        }
   464→        if d.tool:
   465→            entry["tool"] = d.tool
   466→        if d.guidance:
   467→            entry["guidance"] = d.guidance
   468→        result[name] = entry
   469→    return result
   470→

tool_result Bash
```
usage: desloppify plan [-h] [--state STATE] [--output FILE]
                       {show,queue,reset,reorder,describe,note,skip,unskip,reopen,resolve,focus,cluster,triage,commit-log}
                       ...

Manage the living plan — a persistent layer on top of the work queue.
Track custom ordering, clusters, skips, and per-issue annotations.
Run with no subcommand to generate a full prioritized markdown plan.

positional arguments:
  {show,queue,reset,reorder,describe,note,skip,unskip,reopen,resolve,focus,cluster,triage,commit-log}
    show                Show plan metadata summary
    queue               Compact table of upcoming queue items
    reset               Reset plan to empty
    reorder             Reposition issues in the queue
    describe            Set augmented description
    note                Set note on issues
    skip                Skip issues: temporary (default), --permanent
                        (wontfix), or --false-positive
    unskip              Bring skipped issues back to queue (reopens
                        permanent/fp in state)
    reopen              Reopen resolved issues and move back to queue
    resolve             Mark issues as fixed (shows score movement + next
                        step)
    focus               Set or clear active cluster focus
    cluster             Manage issue clusters
    triage              Staged triage workflow for review issues
    commit-log          Track commits and resolved issues for PR updates

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --state STATE         Path to state file
  --output FILE         Write to file instead of stdout

typical workflow:
  desloppify scan                       # detect issues
  desloppify plan                       # full prioritized markdown
  desloppify plan queue                 # compact table of all items
  desloppify plan cluster create ...    # group related issues
  desloppify plan focus <cluster>       # narrow scope
  desloppify next                       # work on the next item
  desloppify plan resolve <id> --attest .. # mark as fixed

patterns (used by reorder, skip, resolve, describe, note, etc.):
  Patterns match issues by detector, file, ID prefix, glob, or name.
  Cluster names also work as patterns — they expand to all member IDs.
  Examples: "security", "src/foo.py", "unused::*React*", "my-cluster"

subcommands:
  show       Show plan metadata summary
  queue      Compact table of upcoming queue items
  reset      Reset plan to empty
  reorder    Reposition issues or clusters in the queue
  resolve    Mark issues as fixed (score movement + next-step)
  describe   Set augmented description
  note       Set note on issues
  skip       Skip issues (temporary/permanent/false_positive)
  unskip     Bring skipped issues back to queue
  reopen     Reopen resolved issues
  focus      Set or clear active cluster focus
  cluster    Manage issue clusters
  triage     Staged triage workflow (after review)
```

usage: desloppify plan skip [-h] [--reason REASON] [--review-after N]
                            [--permanent] [--false-positive] [--note NOTE]
                            [--attest ATTEST]
                            PATTERN [PATTERN ...]

positional arguments:
  PATTERN           Issue ID(s), detector, file path, glob, or cluster name

options:
  -h, --help        show this help message and exit
  --reason REASON   Why this is being skipped
  --review-after N  Re-surface after N scans (temporary only)
  --permanent       Mark as wontfix (score-affecting, requires --note and
                    --attest)
  --false-positive  Mark as false positive (requires --attest)
  --note NOTE       Explanation (required for --permanent)
  --attest ATTEST   Attestation (required for --permanent and --false-
                    positive)

85→def match_issues(
    86→    state: StateModel, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open"
    87→) -> list[dict]:
    88→    """Return issues matching *pattern* with the given status."""
    89→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    90→    return [
    91→        issue
    92→        for issue_id, issue in state["issues"].items()
    93→        if not issue.get("suppressed")
    94→        if (status_filter == "all" or issue["status"] == status_filter)
    95→        and _matches_pattern(issue_id, issue, pattern)
    96→    ]
    97→
    98→
    99→def resolve_issues(
   100→    state: StateModel,
   101→    pattern: str,
   102→    status: str,
   103→    note: str | None = None,
   104→    attestation: str | None = None,
   105→) -> list[str]:
   106→    """Set issue status for matches and return affected issue IDs."""
   107→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   108→    now = utc_now()
   109→    resolved: list[str] = []
   110→    resolved_issues: list[dict] = []
   111→    status_filter = "all" if status == "open" else "open"
   112→    for issue in match_issues(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter):
   113→        previous_status = str(issue.get("status", "open")).strip() or "open"
   114→        if status == "open" and previous_status == "open":
   115→            continue
   116→
   117→        extra_updates: dict[str, object] = {}
   118→        if status == "wontfix":
   119→            snapshot_scan_count = int(state.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   120→            extra_updates["wontfix_scan_count"] = snapshot_scan_count
   121→            extra_updates["wontfix_snapshot"] = {
   122→                "captured_at": now,
   123→                "scan_count": snapshot_scan_count,
   124→                "tier": issue.get("tier"),
   125→                "confidence": issue.get("confidence"),
   126→                "detail": copy.deepcopy(issue.get("detail", {})),
   127→            }
   128→        if status == "open":
   129→            issue["reopen_count"] = int(issue.get("reopen_count", 0) or 0) + 1
   130→            issue.pop("wontfix_scan_count", None)
   131→            issue.pop("wontfix_snapshot", None)
   132→            previous_note = issue.get("note")
   133→            next_note = note if note is not None else previous_note
   134→            extra_updates["resolved_at"] = None
   135→            extra_updates["note"] = next_note
   136→            reopen_attestation = {
   137→                "kind": "manual_reopen",
   138→                "text": attestation or note,
   139→                "attested_at": now,
   140→                "scan_verified": False,
   141→            }
   142→            reopen_attestation["previous_status"] = previous_status
   143→            extra_updates["resolution_attestation"] = reopen_attestation
   144→
   145→        updates: dict[str, object] = {
   146→            "status": status,
   147→            "note": note,
   148→            "resolved_at": now,
   149→            "suppressed": False,
   150→            "suppressed_at": None,
   151→            "suppression_pattern": None,
   152→            "resolution_attestation": {
   153→                "kind": "manual",
   154→                "text": attestation,
   155→                "attested_at": now,
   156→                "scan_verified": False,
   157→            },
   158→        }
   159→        updates.update(extra_updates)
   160→        issue.update(updates)
   161→        resolved.append(issue["id"])
   162→        resolved_issues.append(issue)

    42→def _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
    43→    state: StateModel,
    44→    *,
    45→    status: str,
    46→    resolved_issues: list[dict],
    47→    now: str,
    48→) -> None:
    49→    """Mark subjective assessments as stale when review issues are resolved.
    50→
    51→    The assessment score is preserved (not zeroed) — only a fresh review import
    52→    should change dimension scores.  The stale marker tells the UI to prompt
    53→    for a re-review.
    54→    """
    55→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    56→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    57→        return
    58→
    59→    touched_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    60→    for issue in resolved_issues:
    61→        if issue.get("detector") != "review":
    62→            continue
    63→        dimension = str(issue.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).strip()
    64→        if dimension:
    65→            touched_dimensions.add(dimension)
    66→
    67→    for dimension in sorted(touched_dimensions):
    68→        if dimension not in assessments:
    69→            continue
    70→
    71→        payload = assessments.get(dimension)
    72→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
    73→            payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    74→            payload["refresh_reason"] = f"review_issue_{status}"
    75→            payload["stale_since"] = now
    76→        else:
    77→            assessments[dimension] = {
    78→                "score": coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0,
    79→                "needs_review_refresh": True,
    80→                "refresh_reason": f"review_issue_{status}",
    81→                "stale_since": now,
    82→            }
    83→
    84→

    26→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    27→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    28→
    29→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    30→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    31→    """
    32→    if is_numeric(value):
    33→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    34→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    35→        raw = value.get("score")
    36→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    37→            return None
    38→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    39→    return None
    40→

1→"""State filtering, ignore rules, and issue pattern matching."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→import re
     7→
     8→__all__ = [
     9→    "issue_in_scan_scope",
    10→    "open_scope_breakdown",
    11→    "path_scoped_issues",
    12→    "is_ignored",
    13→    "matched_ignore_pattern",
    14→    "remove_ignored_issues",

    18→
    19→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import rel
    20→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    21→    Issue,
    22→    StateModel,
    23→    ensure_state_defaults,
    24→    utc_now,
    25→    validate_state_invariants,
    26→)
    27→
    28→
    29→def path_scoped_issues(
    30→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→) -> dict[str, Issue]:
    33→    """Filter issues to those within the given scan path."""
    34→    return {
    35→        issue_id: issue
    36→        for issue_id, issue in issues.items()
    37→        if issue_in_scan_scope(str(issue.get("file", "")), scan_path)
    38→    }
    39→
    40→
    41→def issue_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
    42→    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
    43→    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
    44→        return True
    45→    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
    46→    return (
    47→        file_path.startswith(prefix)
    48→        or file_path == scan_path
    49→        or file_path == "."
    50→    )
    51→
    52→
    53→def open_scope_breakdown(
    54→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
    55→    scan_path: str | None,
    56→    *,
    57→    detector: str | None = None,
    58→) -> dict[str, int]:
    59→    """Return open-issue counts split by in-scope vs out-of-scope carryover."""
    60→    in_scope = 0
    61→    out_of_scope = 0
    62→
    63→    for issue in issues.values():
    64→        if issue.get("suppressed"):
    65→            continue
    66→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
    67→            continue
    68→        if detector is not None and issue.get("detector") != detector:
    69→            continue
    70→        file_path = str(issue.get("file", ""))
    71→        if issue_in_scan_scope(file_path, scan_path):
    72→            in_scope += 1
    73→        else:
    74→            out_of_scope += 1
    75→
    76→    return {
    77→        "in_scope": in_scope,
    78→        "out_of_scope": out_of_scope,
    79→        "global": in_scope + out_of_scope,
    80→    }
    81→
    82→
    83→def is_ignored(issue_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]) -> bool:
    84→    """Check if a issue matches any ignore pattern (glob, ID prefix, or file path)."""
    85→    return matched_ignore_pattern(issue_id, file, ignore_patterns) is not None
    86→
    87→
    88→def matched_ignore_pattern(
    89→    issue_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]
    90→) -> str | None:
    91→    """Return the ignore pattern that matched, if any."""
    92→    for pattern in ignore_patterns:
    93→        if "*" in pattern:
    94→            target = issue_id if "::" in pattern else file
    95→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(target, pattern):
    96→                return pattern
    97→            continue
    98→
    99→        if "::" in pattern:
   100→            if issue_id.startswith(pattern):
   101→                return pattern
   102→            continue
   103→
   104→        raw_base = pattern.rstrip("/")
   105→        rel_base = rel(pattern).rstrip("/")
   106→        for base in (raw_base, rel_base):
   107→            if not base:
   108→                continue
   109→            if file == base or file.startswith(base + "/"):
   110→                return pattern
   111→
   112→    return None
   113→
   114→
   115→def remove_ignored_issues(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   116→    """Suppress issues matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
   117→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   118→    matched_ids = [
   119→        issue_id
   120→        for issue_id, issue in state["issues"].items()
   121→        if is_ignored(issue_id, issue["file"], [pattern])
   122→    ]
   123→    now = utc_now()
   124→    for issue_id in matched_ids:
   125→        issue = state["issues"][issue_id]
   126→        issue["suppressed"] = True
   127→        issue["suppressed_at"] = now
   128→        issue["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
   129→    from desloppify.engine._scoring.state_integration import (
   130→        recompute_stats as _recompute_stats,
   131→    )
   132→
   133→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   134→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   135→    return len(matched_ids)
   136→
   137→
   138→def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   139→    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching issues."""
   140→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   141→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   142→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   143→    if pattern not in ignores:
   144→        ignores.append(pattern)
   145→    return remove_ignored_issues(state, pattern)
   146→
   147→
   148→def make_issue(
   149→    detector: str,
   150→    file: str,
   151→    name: str,
   152→    *,
   153→    tier: int,
   154→    confidence: str,
   155→    summary: str,
   156→    detail: dict | None = None,
   157→) -> Issue:
   158→    """Create a normalized issue dict with a stable ID."""
   159→    rfile = rel(file)
   160→    issue_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   161→    now = utc_now()
   162→    return {
   163→        "id": issue_id,
   164→        "detector": detector,
   165→        "file": rfile,
   166→        "tier": tier,
   167→        "confidence": confidence,
   168→        "summary": summary,
   169→        "detail": detail or {},
   170→        "status": "open",
   171→        "note": None,
   172→        "first_seen": now,
   173→        "last_seen": now,
   174→        "resolved_at": None,
   175→        "reopen_count": 0,
   176→    }
   177→
   178→
   179→_HEX8_RE = re.compile(r'^[0-9a-f]{8}$')
   180→
   181→
   182→def _matches_pattern(issue_id: str, issue: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
   183→    """Check if a issue matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, suffix, or path."""
   184→    if issue_id == pattern:
   185→        return True
   186→    if "*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(issue_id, pattern):
   187→        return True
   188→    if "::" in pattern and issue_id.startswith(pattern):
   189→        return True
   190→    if _HEX8_RE.match(pattern) and issue_id.endswith("::" + pattern):
   191→        return True
   192→    if (
   193→        issue.get("detector") == pattern
   194→        or issue["file"] == pattern
   195→        or issue["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   196→    ):
   197→        return True
   198→
   199→    # Name-segment fallback: bare name matches the last ::segment of the ID
   200→    if "::" not in pattern and "::" in issue_id:
   201→        segments = issue_id.split("::")
   202→        name_segment = segments[-1]
   203→        if name_segment == pattern:
   204→            return True
   205→        # For hashed IDs, also match the descriptive name (second-to-last segment)
   206→        if len(segments) >= 3 and _HEX8_RE.match(name_segment) and segments[-2] == pattern:
   207→            return True
   208→
   209→    return False
   210→

1→"""Skip/unskip mutations for plan operations."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.operations_queue import _remove_id_from_lists
     6→from desloppify.engine._plan.promoted_ids import prune_promoted_ids
     7→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel, SkipEntry, ensure_plan_defaults
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.skip_policy import skip_kind_needs_state_reopen
     9→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    10→
    11→
    12→def skip_items(
    13→    plan: PlanModel,
    14→    issue_ids: list[str],
    15→    *,
    16→    kind: str = "temporary",
    17→    reason: str | None = None,
    18→    note: str | None = None,
    19→    attestation: str | None = None,
    20→    review_after: int | None = None,
    21→    scan_count: int = 0,
    22→) -> int:
    23→    """Move issue IDs to the skipped dict. Returns count skipped."""
    24→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    25→    now = utc_now()
    26→    count = 0
    27→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
    28→    skip_set = set(issue_ids)
    29→    prune_promoted_ids(plan, skip_set)
    30→    for fid in issue_ids:
    31→        _remove_id_from_lists(plan, fid)
    32→        skipped[fid] = {
    33→            "issue_id": fid,
    34→            "kind": kind,
    35→            "reason": reason,
    36→            "note": note,
    37→            "attestation": attestation,
    38→            "created_at": now,
    39→            "review_after": review_after,
    40→            "skipped_at_scan": scan_count,
    41→        }
    42→        count += 1
    43→    return count
    44→
    45→
    46→def unskip_items(
    47→    plan: PlanModel, issue_ids: list[str]
    48→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
    49→    """Bring issue IDs back from skipped to the end of queue_order.
    50→
    51→    Returns ``(count_unskipped, permanent_ids_needing_state_reopen)``
    52→    where the second list contains IDs that were permanent or false_positive
    53→    and need their state-layer status reopened by the caller.
    54→    """
    55→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    56→    count = 0
    57→    need_reopen: list[str] = []
    58→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
    59→    for fid in issue_ids:
    60→        entry = skipped.pop(fid, None)
    61→        if entry is not None:
    62→            if skip_kind_needs_state_reopen(str(entry.get("kind", ""))):
    63→                need_reopen.append(fid)
    64→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
    65→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
    66→            count += 1
    67→    return count, need_reopen
    68→
    69→
    70→def resurface_stale_skips(
    71→    plan: PlanModel, current_scan_count: int
    72→) -> list[str]:
    73→    """Move temporary skips past their review_after threshold back to queue.
    74→
    75→    Returns list of resurfaced issue IDs.
    76→    """
    77→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    78→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
    79→    resurfaced: list[str] = []
    80→    for fid in list(skipped):
    81→        entry = skipped[fid]
    82→        if entry.get("kind") != "temporary":
    83→            continue
    84→        review_after = entry.get("review_after")
    85→        if review_after is None:
    86→            continue
    87→        skipped_at = entry.get("skipped_at_scan", 0)
    88→        if current_scan_count >= skipped_at + review_after:
    89→            skipped.pop(fid)
    90→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
    91→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
    92→            resurfaced.append(fid)
    93→    return resurfaced
    94→
    95→
    96→__all__ = ["resurface_stale_skips", "skip_items", "unskip_items"]
    97→

## Task 1: Plan Queue Curation -- CLI Command Sequence

### Current State

The queue has 268 items. The top 149 are all `structural` detector items, followed by 76 `responsibility_cohesion` items. This means the first 225 items are all structural noise, burying the real bugs.

### Phase 1: Skip the noise (detector-level patterns first)

The `plan skip` command accepts detector names as patterns (confirmed via `_matches_pattern` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py` line 193: `issue.get("detector") == pattern`). This means `plan skip structural` will match ALL 149 structural items in one command, regardless of whether they are clustered or unclustered.

**Important observation**: The user listed `auto/responsibility_cohesion-render.py` and `auto/responsibility_cohesion-scope.py` as SKIP targets (4 items), but there are 76 total `responsibility_cohesion` items in the queue. The other 72 are unclustered. These are all "Mixed responsibilities: N top-level funcs across M concerns" messages -- the same structural-noise pattern. I recommend skipping the entire `responsibility_cohesion` detector, not just the 2 named clusters.

Execute in this order:

```bash
# 1. Skip ALL structural items (149 items) -- detector pattern matches all
desloppify plan skip structural --reason "Files 300-800 LOC are normal; mechanical splitting creates facade wrappers"

# 2. Skip ALL responsibility_cohesion items (76 items) -- same structural noise
desloppify plan skip responsibility_cohesion --reason "Orchestration modules legitimately touch many concerns"

# 3. Skip noise clusters by cluster name
desloppify plan skip auto/smells-swallowed_error --reason "Intentional error handling patterns"
desloppify plan skip auto/smells-silent_except --reason "Deliberate robustness patterns"
desloppify plan skip auto/smells-noop_function --reason "Protocol stubs, not dead code"
desloppify plan skip auto/smells-high_cyclomatic_complexity --reason "Inherent complexity in parsers/dispatchers, not decomposable"
desloppify plan skip auto/smells-deferred_import --reason "Deliberate circular-import workarounds"
desloppify plan skip auto/smells-optional_param_sprawl --reason "Framework code legitimately has many params"
desloppify plan skip auto/smells-annotation_quality --reason "Low-value type annotation tweaks"
desloppify plan skip auto/security --reason "Mostly Bandit noise: subprocess calls in a CLI tool"
desloppify plan skip auto/private_imports --reason "0 visible members, unclear state"
desloppify plan skip auto/flat_dirs-overload --reason "Mostly noise"

# 4. Skip manual clusters that are empty, duplicative, or deferred
desloppify plan skip annotation-quality --reason "Duplicates auto/smells-annotation_quality; skip both"
desloppify plan skip test-coverage --reason "Important but lower priority than bugs; revisit after bug fixes"
desloppify plan skip auto/test_coverage --reason "Same as test-coverage; deferred"
desloppify plan skip genuinely-poor --reason "Empty cluster"
desloppify plan skip dead-code-cleanup --reason "Empty cluster"
```

**Note on `error-handling` cluster overlap**: The `error-handling` manual cluster contains 13 items from the `smells` detector with subtype `swallowed_error`. The `auto/smells-swallowed_error` cluster contains the same 13 items. Skipping `auto/smells-swallowed_error` by cluster name will skip those specific issue IDs. When we later try to reorder `error-handling` to the top, its members will already be in the skipped set. 

To avoid this conflict: skip `auto/smells-swallowed_error` AFTER reordering `error-handling` to the top, OR skip it using a more targeted pattern that excludes the error-handling members. The simpler approach: **do NOT skip `auto/smells-swallowed_error` separately** since the error-handling cluster already curates those same items. The 13 items in `auto/smells-swallowed_error` are the same 13 in `error-handling`.

**Revised step 3** -- remove `auto/smells-swallowed_error` from the skip list since its members ARE the error-handling cluster.

## Task 2: Tone Down Structural Issue Presentation -- Code Edits

### Edit 1: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/base/structural.py`

**Line 61** -- Change the summary prefix from prescriptive to advisory:

```python
# BEFORE (line 61):
summary = "Needs decomposition: " + " / ".join(signals)

# AFTER:
summary = "Large file: " + " / ".join(signals)
```

Rationale: "Large file" is descriptive/factual. It reports what was detected without commanding action. This pairs well with `needs_judgment=True` since it leaves the decision to the developer. "Consider splitting" was another option but still implies action. "Large file" is the most neutral -- it states the observation and lets the developer decide.

### Edit 2: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/cluster_strategy.py`

**Lines 88-92** -- Change the `generate_description` structural branch:

```python
# BEFORE (lines 88-92):
if detector == "structural":
    files = {os.path.basename(member.get("file", "")) for member in members}
    if len(files) == 1:
        return f"Decompose {next(iter(files))}"
    return f"Decompose {count} large files"

# AFTER:
if detector == "structural":
    files = {os.path.basename(member.get("file", "")) for member in members}
    if len(files) == 1:
        return f"Review file size: {next(iter(files))}"
    return f"Review {count} large files"
```

### Edit 3: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/base/registry.py`

```python

"decompose large files — extract logic into focused modules",

# AFTER:

```

### Edit 4: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_lang_base.py`

**Line 184** -- Update the test assertion to match the new summary prefix:

```python
# BEFORE (line 184):

# AFTER:
assert "Large file" in results[0]["summary"]
```
